Material support now also includes diagonal `AnisotropicMedium`,\nexplicit defer-policy surfaces for `FullyAnisotropicMedium` and `Medium2D`, per-axis\ncoefficient preparation, and staged anisotropic constitutive-update helpers that mix\nscalar, dispersive, and clamp branches across `x`, `y`, and `z`. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_function_67vttoazb4fq_1","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Phase 1 Chunk Contract and Multi-Node Decomposition Architecture\n2\t\n3\t## Status\n4\t\n5\t**Draft — for review and task handoff**\n6\t\n7\t## 1. Background and Design Motivation\n8\t\n9\tPhase 1 (`task-047`) establishes the architectural contract for chunk-based decomposition before solver kernels are wired to a runtime executor. The goal is to define what a chunk owns, how chunks expose halo and monitor metadata, how chunk placement maps onto devices or nodes, and how this avoids blocking later systolic or overlapped execution phases.\n10\t\n11\t### Reference Foundations\n12\t\n13\t- **Meep chunk architecture** (`../meep/doc/docs/Chunks_and_Symmetry.md`): chunk is the stable decomposition unit, not MPI rank. A rank may own multiple chunks. Chunk boundaries align with PML layers and symmetry planes, not with MPI ranks. Communication plans are stage-specific and component-family-specific.\n14\t- **Systolic FDTD scheduling** (`papers/systolic_fdtd.pdf`): the important architectural seam is the split between chunk-local interior work, lower-dimensional boundary exchange, and a pluggable execution policy. Per-face, per-stage halo semantics and interior-versus-boundary planning must be explicit now so future systolic or overlapped execution can be added without redesign.\n15\t- **Phase 1 codebase state**: `CompiledSimulation` in `compiler/pipeline.py` treats the entire grid as one monolithic entity. `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec` is a global constant. No chunk index, rank, ownership mask, or per-chunk field decomposition exists yet.\n16\t\n17\t### Design Principles\n18\t\n19\t1. **Chunk is the unit of parallelism.** Rank or device is placement, not computation.\n20\t2. **Logical and stored topology diverge.** Symmetry reduction and periodic/Bloch remapping are logical-address transforms over a full-domain view, not data-layout changes.\n21\t3. **Scene compilation is separate from stepping.** Geometry and material initialization produce coefficient fields; those fields are then chunked for execution.\n22\t4. **Bulk-synchronous by default, systolic-ready by design.** Phase 1 is bulk-synchronous, but chunk metadata and runtime orchestration preserve per-face, per-stage halo semantics so overlap can be layered in later without rearchitecting the chunk contract.\n23\t5. **Halo depth is per-face, not global.** Different boundary families (PML, ABC, Bloch, periodic) require different halo widths and different exchange semantics.\n24\t\n25\t---\n26\t\n27\t## 2. Chunk Data Model\n28\t\n29\t### 2.1 ChunkSpec — Planning Metadata\n30\t\n31\t`ChunkSpec` is a frozen planning metadata object produced during scene compilation (not a runtime data structure). It describes the intended decomposition before any field buffers are allocated.\n32\t\n33\t```python\n34\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n35\tclass ChunkSpec:\n36\t    \"\"\"Planning metadata for one chunk — produced during compilation.\"\"\"\n37\t\n38\t    chunk_index: tuple[int, int, int]          # logical position in chunk grid\n39\t    global_bounds: tuple[Vec3, Vec3]            # full-domain bounds of this chunk (lo, hi)\n40\t    interior_bounds: tuple[Vec3, Vec3]          # interior (non-halo) cell index range\n41\t    owned_bounds: tuple[Vec3, Vec3]              # cells this chunk updates (including boundary)\n42\t    local_grid_shape: tuple[int, int, int]       # full local array shape incl. ghost cells\n43\t\n44\t    # Per-face halo metadata\n45\t    face_halos: dict[str, FaceHalo]              # key: \"x.minus\", \"x.plus\", \"y.minus\", ...\n46\t\n47\t    # Symmetry metadata\n48\t    is_reduced: bool                             # True if chunk is in symmetry-reduced region\n49\t    symmetry_multiplicity: int                   # how many full-domain points this chunk represents\n50\t\n51\t    # Ownership flags for sources and monitors\n52\t    source_indices: tuple[int, ...]             # indices into compiled_sources owned here\n53\t    monitor_indices: tuple[int, ...]              # number of ghost cells on this face\n72\t    neighbor_chunk_index: tuple[int, int, int] | None  # None = domain boundary\n73\t    exchange_kind: Literal[\n74\t        \"periodic\", \"bloch\", \"pec\", \"pmc\",\n75\t        \"pml\", \"stable_pml\", \"absorber\", \"abc\",\n76\t        \"symmetry_copy\", \"interior\"\n77\t    ]\n78\t    phase_factor: complex = 1.0 + 0.0j          # for Bloch: e^{i k · d}\n79\t    electric_signs: tuple[int, int, int] = (1, 1, 1)   # for PEC/PMC/symmetry reflection\n80\t    magnetic_signs: tuple[int, int, int] = (1, 1, 1)   # for PEC/PMC/symmetry reflection"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"4e376622-ce48-4a0e-9b7d-da84d1aac604","uuid":"b9b1eec1-5d3e-4bae-a09b-cb73e02b9d4f","timestamp":"2026-04-07T13:11:07.500Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/workspace/autofdtd/phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md","content":"# Phase 1 Chunk Contract and Multi-Node Decomposition Architecture\n\n## Status\n\n**Draft — for review and task handoff**\n\n## 1. Background and Design Motivation\n\nPhase 1 (`task-047`) establishes the architectural contract for chunk-based decomposition before solver kernels are wired to a runtime executor. The goal is to define what a chunk owns, how chunks expose halo and monitor metadata, how chunk placement maps onto devices or nodes, and how this avoids blocking later systolic or overlapped execution phases.\n\n### Reference Foundations\n\n- **Meep chunk architecture** (`../meep/doc/docs/Chunks_and_Symmetry.md`): chunk is the stable decomposition unit, not MPI rank. A rank may own multiple chunks. Chunk boundaries align with PML layers and symmetry planes, not with MPI ranks. Communication plans are stage-specific and component-family-specific.\n- **Systolic FDTD scheduling** (`papers/systolic_fdtd.pdf`): the important architectural seam is the split between chunk-local interior work, lower-dimensional boundary exchange, and a pluggable execution policy. Per-face, per-stage halo semantics and interior-versus-boundary planning must be explicit now so future systolic or overlapped execution can be added without redesign.\n- **Phase 1 codebase state**: `CompiledSimulation` in `compiler/pipeline.py` treats the entire grid as one monolithic entity. `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec` is a global constant. No chunk index, rank, ownership mask, or per-chunk field decomposition exists yet.\n\n### Design Principles\n\n1. **Chunk is the unit of parallelism.** Rank or device is placement, not computation.\n2. **Logical and stored topology diverge.** Symmetry reduction and periodic/Bloch remapping are logical-address transforms over a full-domain view, not data-layout changes.\n3. **Scene compilation is separate from stepping.** Geometry and material initialization produce coefficient fields; those fields are then chunked for execution.\n4. **Bulk-synchronous by default, systolic-ready by design.** Phase 1 is bulk-synchronous, but chunk metadata and runtime orchestration preserve per-face, per-stage halo semantics so overlap can be layered in later without rearchitecting the chunk contract.\n5. **Halo depth is per-face, not global.** Different boundary families (PML, ABC, Bloch, periodic) require different halo widths and different exchange semantics.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Chunk Data Model\n\n### 2.1 ChunkSpec — Planning Metadata\n\n`ChunkSpec` is a frozen planning metadata object produced during scene compilation (not a runtime data structure). It describes the intended decomposition before any field buffers are allocated.\n\n```python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ChunkSpec:\n    \"\"\"Planning metadata for one chunk — produced during compilation.\"\"\"\n\n    chunk_index: tuple[int, int, int]          # logical position in chunk grid\n    global_bounds: tuple[Vec3, Vec3]            # full-domain bounds of this chunk (lo, hi)\n    interior_bounds: tuple[Vec3, Vec3]          # interior (non-halo) cell index range\n    owned_bounds: tuple[Vec3, Vec3]              # cells this chunk updates (including boundary)\n    local_grid_shape: tuple[int, int, int]       # full local array shape incl. ghost cells\n\n    # Per-face halo metadata\n    face_halos: dict[str, FaceHalo]              # key: \"x.minus\", \"x.plus\", \"y.minus\", ...\n\n    # Symmetry metadata\n    is_reduced: bool                             # True if chunk is in symmetry-reduced region\n    symmetry_multiplicity: int                   # how many full-domain points this chunk represents\n\n    # Ownership flags for sources and monitors\n    source_indices: tuple[int, ...]             # indices into compiled_sources owned here\n    monitor_indices: tuple[int, ...]              # indices into compiled_monitors owned here\n```\n\n**Key invariants:**\n- `interior_bounds` defines the region this chunk updates without reading halo data.\n- `owned_bounds` includes the one-cell-wide boundary ring that this chunk writes and other chunks read.\n- `local_grid_shape` is the shape of the full local array including ghost cells — this is what field buffers are allocated to.\n- For a chunk at chunk_index `(i, j, k)`, the global cell index range of its interior is `[interior_bounds.lo, interior_bounds.hi)` in grid coordinates.\n\n### 2.2 FaceHalo — Per-Face Halo Descriptor\n\n```python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass FaceHalo:\n    \"\"\"Halo metadata for one face of one chunk.\"\"\"\n\n    axis: Literal[\"x\", \"y\", \"z\"]\n    side: Literal[\"minus\", \"plus\"]\n    depth: int                                  # number of ghost cells on this face\n    neighbor_chunk_index: tuple[int, int, int] | None  # None = domain boundary\n    exchange_kind: Literal[\n        \"periodic\", \"bloch\", \"pec\", \"pmc\",\n        \"pml\", \"stable_pml\", \"absorber\", \"abc\",\n        \"symmetry_copy\", \"interior\"\n    ]\n    phase_factor: complex = 1.0 + 0.0j          # for Bloch: e^{i k · d}\n    electric_signs: tuple[int, int, int] = (1, 1, 1)   # for PEC/PMC/symmetry reflection\n    magnetic_signs: tuple[int, int, int] = (1, 1, 1)   # for PEC/PMC/symmetry reflection","numLines":80,"startLine":1,"totalLines":393}}}

monitors, boundary and grid references, symmetry, and version tagging.\n41\t\n42\tThese models are frozen, tagged, and JSON-ready so later tasks can lower them into a\n43\tseparate execution IR without redesigning the public container layer.\n44\t\n45\t`autofdtd.core.validation` now provides the first checklist-aware normalization layer for\n46\tthese containers. It trims names, normalizes basic vector payloads, validates primitive\n47\tgeometry constraints when enough shape data is present, rejects deferred or rejected\n48\tfeature tags explicitly, warns when known primitive bounds extend outside the simulation\n49\tdomain, and coerces zero-thickness absorbing boundaries to `Periodic` so later compiler\n50\twork does not inherit ambiguous input states.\n51\t\n52\tThe grid namespace now adds a separate manual-discretization seam. `GridSpec` resolves\n53\tthree axis-local grid models into a `ResolvedGrid` object without entangling that work\n54\twith runtime scheduling. That keeps scene modeling, mesh planning, and execution as\n55\tdistinct subsystems while giving later compiler and mode-solver tasks a concrete domain\n56\tdiscretization contract to consume.\n57\t\n58\t`autofdtd.grid.subpixel` now carries the first explicit interface-materialization policy\n59\tsurface. `Simulation.subpixel` accepts `bool` values using Tidy3D-style coercion\n60\t(`True` -> default `SubpixelSpec()`, `False` -> `SubpixelSpec.staircasing()`) plus an\n61\texplicit `SubpixelSpec` object. Phase 1 keeps the policy set narrow: dielectric\n62\tinterfaces can use `PolarizedAveraging` or `Staircasing`, while metal, PEC, PMC, and\n63\tlossy-metal interfaces remain explicit staircasing-only buckets until anisotropic media\n64\tand heavier conformal treatments land.\n65\t\n66\tThe material namespace now has a real Phase 1 foothold. `Medium`, `PECMedium`, and\n67\t`PMCMedium` are typed public models, lower into typed IR, compile into scalar\n68\tconstitutive coefficients, and feed a staged electric/magnetic constitutive-update path\n69\tin `autofdtd.kernels.materials`. That keeps material validation, scene assignment,\n70\tcoefficient preparation, and runtime updates as distinct seams instead of hiding them in\n71\tstructure dict payloads.\n72\t\n73\tThe boundary namespace now has the first runtime-meaningful boundary subset. `Periodic`,\n74\t`PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary`, `Boundary`, and `BoundarySpec` are typed public models;\n75\t`autofdtd.ir` lowers them into typed boundary IR; `autofdtd.compiler.boundaries`\n76\tcompiles them into per-face halo metadata; and `autofdtd.kernels.boundaries` applies\n77\tperiodic-copy or conductor-reflection ghost updates without folding boundary behavior\n78\tinto the constitutive kernels.\n79\t\n80\tThe runtime namespace now also has an explicit stop-policy seam separate from the\n81\tfuture Maxwell update loop. `Simulation` exposes derived `scaled_courant`,\n82\t`time_step_size()`, and `num_time_steps()` helpers; `autofdtd.compiler.runtime`\n83\tcompiles those controls into a `CompiledRuntimeControls` record; and\n84\t`autofdtd.runtime.controls` evaluates run-time, step-count, and shutoff-based early\n85\ttermination without coupling that policy to any particular chunk scheduler.\n86\t\n87\t## Execution IR\n88\t\n89\t`autofdtd.ir` now defines a versioned transport layer around the public containers:\n90\t\n91\t- `ComponentIR` is the family-tagged envelope for geometry, medium, source, monitor,\n92\t  boundary, grid, and subpixel payloads.\n93\t- `StructureIR`, `SceneIR`, and `SimulationIR` preserve ordered scene semantics while\n94\t  remaining independent from the public API module layout.\n95\t- `ExecutionManifestIR` and `ExecutionPackageIR` provide a bundle contract for local or\n96\t  remote execution packaging with a manifest, an entrypoint payload, and hashed artifact\n97\t  metadata.\n98\t\n99\tThe initial lowering path is snapshot-oriented rather than compilation-oriented. typed-object problem, not a loose dict problem.\n- Preserve room for chunk-local interior stages, boundary stages, and future overlap-aware scheduling.\n- Make unsupported features explicit through validation or feature-matrix policy.\n\n## Current Backbone\n\nThe first public models live in `autofdtd.api` and `autofdtd.core`:\n\n- `Structure` carries `geometry`, `medium`, optional `name`, finite-difference time-domain solver scaffold, aligned to a Tidy3D-inspired\npublic API surface and a chunk-based multi-node-ready runtime architecture.\n\n## Current Status: Phase 1 Complete\n\nPhase 1 has produced a pip-installable, end-to-end-runnable FDTD package with:\n\n- **100 registered test functions** across boundary, material, source, monitor, kernel, and integration coverage\n- **73 implemented features** spanning core containers, geometry, materials, boundaries, sources, monitors, and postprocessing\n- **17 deferred features** with explicit deferral rationale and no silent pipeline** from public API → SimulationIR → ExecutionPackageIR → CompiledSimulation → runtime execution\n- **Chunk architecture contract** (`phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md`) ready for multi-GPU |\n| Materials | 9 | 5 | 8 |\n| Boundaries | 10 | 1 | 0 |\n| Sources | 14 | 0 | 1 |\n| Monitors | 17 | 0 | 0 |\n| Postprocessing | 2 | 1 | 0 |\n| **Total** | **73** | **17** | **10** |\n\n## Quick entrypoints\n  core/         — frozen model base, validation, container semantics\n  geometry/     — Box, Sphere, Cylinder, PolySlab, composite wrappers\n  materials/    — Medium, PEC/PMC, PoleResidue, Sellmeier, Lorentz,\n                  Drude, Debye, AnisotropicMedium\n  boundaries/   — Periodic, PEC/PMC, Bloch, PML, StablePML, Absorber, ABC\n  sources/      — GaussianPulse, ContinuousWave, UniformCurrentSource,\n                  PointDipole, PlaneWave, GaussianBeam, TFSF, ModeSource\n  monitors/     — FieldMonitor, FluxMonitor, ModeMonitor, MediumMonitor,\n                  Projection monitors, SurfaceFieldMonitor\n  grid/         — UniformGrid, CustomGrid, AutoGrid, GridSpec, SubpixelSpec\n  modes/        — ModeSpec, ModeSolverCrossSection, ModeSolution\n  ir/           — tagged IR models (SimulationIR, SceneIR, StructureIR, etc.)\n  compiler/     — scene compilation, material coefficient planning,\n                  source/monitor compilation, boundary planning, chunk layout\n  runtime/      — timestep loop, convergence control, monitor recording,\n                  halo exchange, near-to-far\n  kernels/      — Warp kernel conventions, Maxwell update stages,\n                  material update kernels, source injection, boundary kernels\n  diagnostics/  — RuntimeProgressLogger, RuntimeLogEvent, metrics collection\n  examples/     — validation.py (10 examples), integration.py, near2far.py\n```\n\n## Public API Entrypoints\n\n```python\nfrom autofdtd.api import (\n    compile_simulation,      # Simulation → CompiledSimulation\n    simulation_to_ir,        # Simulation → SimulationIR\n    simulation_to_execution_package,  # Simulation → ExecutionPackageIR\n    run_until_stop,          # CompiledSimulation → ExecutionResult\n    run_until_stop_with_logging,  # with structured log events\n)\n```\n\n## Unsupported-Feature Policy\n\nPhase 1 implements explicit reject and defer behavior rather than silent gaps:\n\n- **Reject**: `Custom*` media families, `lumped_elements`, `MicrowaveTerminalSource`,\n  `InternalAbsorber`. Attempting to use these raises `AutoFDTDNotImplementedError`\n  with a clear message about what Phase 1 covers.\n\n- **Defer**: `TriangleMesh`, `QuasiUniformGrid`, `FullyAnisotropicMedium`, `Medium2D`,\n  `PerturbationMedium`, `LossyMetalMedium`, `ModeABCBoundary`, and others. These are\n  parsed, validated, and documented with deferral rationale in `planning.py`. Attempting\n  to use a deferred feature raises `AutoFDTDNotImplementedError` with the specific deferral\n  context.\n\n- **Deferred by dependency**: `ModeSource` and `ModeMonitor` depend on the mode solver\n  being available. The mode solver (`autofdtd.modes`) is implemented and runnable, but\n  full mode-source injection integration is deferred pending chunk kernel wiring.\n\n## Architecture\n\nPhase 1 separates scene modeling, scene compilation, runtime stepping, and IO:\n\n```\nSimulation → simulation_to_ir() → SimulationIR\nSimulationIR + compile_simulation() → CompiledSimulation\nCompiledSimulation → run_until_stop() → ExecutionResult\n```\n\n- `Simulation` is the public API object model (frozen, tagged, JSON-ready)\n- `SimulationIR` is the versioned execution transport (schema: `phase1.v1`)\n- `CompiledSimulation` holds resolved grid, scene coefficients, compiled sources/monitors/boundaries, and chunk layout\n- `ExecutionResult` captures field state, step count, stop reason, integrated history, monitor data, and runtime metrics\n\nChunk decomposition is designed around `ChunkSpec`, `FaceHalo`, and `ChunkLayout`\ncontracts. Phase 1 is monolithic (single chunk, `num_chunks=(1,1,1)`) but the chunk\ncontract is ready for multi-GPU and MPI scaling. Per-face halo depth is per-face, not\nglobal; PML, ABC, Bloch, periodic, and symmetry halos are all represented.\n\n## Development\n\n```bash\npytest                              # run all tests\nruff check .                       # lint\nmypy src                           # type check\nautofdtd-feature-matrix --format table  # show feature status table\npython -c \"from autofdtd.examples.validation import *; features** spanning core containers, geometry, materials, boundaries, sources, monitors, and postprocessing\n- **17 deferred features** with explicit deferral rationale and no silent gaps\n- **10 rejected features** with clear error messaging so users know what is out of scope\n- **9 canonical validation examples** exercising compile → execute → validate workflows\n- **IR lowering pipeline** from public API → SimulationIR → ExecutionPackageIR → CompiledSimulation → runtime execution\n- **Chunk architecture contract** (`phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md`) ready for multi-GPU and MPI scaling\n\n## Feature Coverage Summary\n\n| Family | Implement | Defer | Reject |\n|--------|-----------|-------|--------|\n| Core | 4 | 2 | 1 |\n| Geometry | 7 | 2 | 0 |\n| Grid | 10 | 6 | 0 |\n| Materials | 9 | 5 | 8 |\n| Boundaries | 10 | 1 | 0 |\n| Sources | 14 | 0 | 1 |\n| Monitors | 17 | 0 | 0 |\n| Postprocessing | 2 | 1 | 0 |\n| **Total** | **73** | **17** | **10** |\n\n## Quick Start\n\n```bash\npython -m pip install -e .[dev]\npytest\nautofdtd-feature-matrix --format table\n```\n\n## Package Layout\n\n```\nautofdtd/\n  api/          — public Simulation, Scene, Structure entrypoints\n  core/         — frozen model base, validation, container semantics\n  geometry/     — Box, Sphere, Cylinder, PolySlab, composite wrappers\n  materials/    — Medium, PEC/PMC, PoleResidue, Sellmeier, Lorentz,\n                  Drude, Debye, AnisotropicMedium\n  boundaries/   — Periodic, PEC/PMC, Bloch, PML, StablePML, Absorber, ABC\n  sources/      — GaussianPulse, ContinuousWave, UniformCurrentSource,\n                  PointDipole, PlaneWave, GaussianBeam, TFSF, ModeSource\n  monitors/     — FieldMonitor, FluxMonitor, ModeMonitor, MediumMonitor,\n                  Projection monitors, SurfaceFieldMonitor\n  grid/         — UniformGrid, CustomGrid, AutoGrid, GridSpec, SubpixelSpec\n  modes/        — ModeSpec, ModeSolverCrossSection, ModeSolution\n  ir/           — tagged IR models (SimulationIR, SceneIR, StructureIR, etc.)\n  compiler/     — scene compilation, material coefficient planning,\n                  source/monitor compilation, boundary planning, chunk layout\n  runtime/      — timestep loop, convergence control, monitor recording,\n                  halo exchange, near-to-far\n  kernels/      — Warp kernel conventions, Maxwell update stages,\n                  material update kernels, source injection, boundary kernels\n  diagnostics/  — RuntimeProgressLogger, RuntimeLogEvent, metrics collection\n  examples/     — validation.py (10 examples), integration.py, near2far.py\n```\n\n## Public API Entrypoints\n\n```python\nfrom autofdtd.api import (\n    compile_simulation,      # Simulation → CompiledSimulation\n    simulation_to_ir,        # Simulation → SimulationIR\n    simulation_to_execution_package,  # Simulation → ExecutionPackageIR\n    run_until_stop,          # CompiledSimulation → ExecutionResult\n    run_until_stop_with_logging,  # with structured log events\n)\n```\n\n## Unsupported-Feature Policy\n\nPhase 1 implements explicit reject and defer behavior rather than silent gaps:\n\n- **Reject**: `Custom*` media families, `lumped_elements`, `MicrowaveTerminalSource`,\n  `InternalAbsorber`. Attempting to use these raises `AutoFDTDNotImplementedError`\n  with a clear message about what Phase 1 covers.\n\n- **Defer**: `TriangleMesh`, `QuasiUniformGrid`, `FullyAnisotropicMedium`, `Medium2D`,\n  `PerturbationMedium`, `LossyMetalMedium`, `ModeABCBoundary`, and others. These are\n  parsed, validated, and documented with deferral rationale in `planning.py`. Attempting\n  to use a deferred feature raises `AutoFDTDNotImplementedError` with the specific deferral\n  context.\n\n- **Deferred by dependency**: `ModeSource` and `ModeMonitor` depend on the mode solver\n  being available. The mode solver (`autofdtd.modes`) is implemented and runnable, but\n  full mode-source injection integration is deferred pending chunk kernel wiring.\n\n## Architecture\n\nPhase 1 separates scene modeling, scene compilation, runtime stepping, and IO:\n\n```\nSimulation → simulation_to_ir() → SimulationIR\nSimulationIR + compile_simulation() → CompiledSimulation\nCompiledSimulation → run_until_stop() → ExecutionResult\n```\n\n- `Simulation` is the public API object model (frozen, tagged, JSON-ready)\n- `SimulationIR` is the versioned execution transport (schema: `phase1.v1`)\n- `CompiledSimulation` holds resolved grid, scene coefficients, compiled sources/monitors/boundaries, and chunk layout\n- `ExecutionResult` captures field state, step count, stop reason, integrated history, monitor data, and runtime metrics\n\nChunk decomposition is designed around `ChunkSpec`, `FaceHalo`, and `ChunkLayout`\ncontracts. Phase 1 is monolithic (single chunk, `num_chunks=(1,1,1)`) but the chunk\ncontract is ready for multi-GPU and MPI scaling. Per-face halo depth is per-face, not\nglobal; PML, ABC, Bloch, periodic, and symmetry halos are all represented.\n\n## Development\n\n```bash\npytest                              # run all tests\nruff check .                       # lint\nmypy src                           # type check\nautofdtd-feature-matrix --format table  # show feature status table\npython -c \"from autofdtd.examples.validation import *; ...\"  # run validation examples\n```\n\n## Phase 1 Evidence Base\n\n- **751 registered test functions** across the test suite\n- **9 canonical validation examples** in `src/autofdtd/examples/validation.py`\n- **9 integration examples** in `src/autofdtd/examples/integration.py`\n- **3 Warp kernel convention modules** in `src/autofdtd/kernels/backend.py`\n- **End-to-end execution** through `run_compiled_simulation()` producing\n  `ExecutionResult` with field state, metrics, and monitor data\n- **IR transport** from public API through tagged IR to execution package\n\n## What's Ready for Hand-Off\n\nThe following are production-ready for Phase 1 developer validation:\n\n1. **Public API**: Simulation, Scene, Structure, all geometry/material/source/monitor models\n2. **IR lowering**: Full `simulation_to_ir()` pipeline with schema versioning\n3. **Compilation pipeline**: `compile_simulation()` producing frozen `CompiledSimulation`\n4. **Runtime execution**: `run_until_stop()` / `run_until_stop_with_logging()` with convergence\n5. **Validation examples**: 10 canonical physical scenarios with expected outcomes\n6. **Chunk architecture**: Full contract documented in `phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md`\n7. **Feature matrix**: `autofdtd.planning._FEATURE_MATRIX` with 100 entries\n\n## What Needs Further Validation\n\n1. **Warp kernel execution**: Warp not currently installed; NumPy backend used for tests\n2. **Large-scale performance**: Gcells/s metrics need GPU hardware and realistic problem sizes\n3. **MPI multi-node scaling**: Chunk layout is defined; halo exchange is stubbed for single-chunk\n4. **Mode source integration**: Mode solver works; mode-source injection in timestep loop deferred","originalFile":"# AutoFDTD\n\nGPU-first finite-difference time-domain solver scaffolding for Phase 1 Maxwell work.\n\nThe repository now provides a pip-installable `src/` layout package, subsystem-oriented\nmodule namespaces, development tooling, test entrypoints, a feature-matrix CLI, and\ndocumentation scaffolding aligned with the local Tidy3D EM feature checklist.\n\n## Package Layout\n\nThe package is intentionally split along Phase 1 subsystem boundaries:\n\n- `autofdtd.api`: public object-model entrypoints and compatibility planning surfaces\n- `autofdtd.core`: shared model and tagging foundations\n- `autofdtd.geometry`, `materials`, `boundaries`, `sources`, `monitors`, `grid`, `modes`\n- `autofdtd.ir`: tagged execution-IR namespace\n- `autofdtd.compiler`: scene-lowering and planning namespace\n- `autofdtd.runtime`: chunk orchestration and execution namespace\n- `autofdtd.kernels`: Warp kernel conventions and backend staging namespace\n- `autofdtd.diagnostics`: logging, metrics, and validation namespace\n\nThe package does not yet claim solver completeness. It now includes the first tagged,\nimmutable container models for `Simulation`, `Scene`, and `Structure`, with ordered\nstructure precedence helpers and JSON-ready serialization for later IR lowering. The\n`autofdtd.ir` namespace now also exposes a versioned execution package contract that can\nsnapshot a simulation into `simulation.json` plus a hashed `manifest.json` for local or\nremote execution handoff. Shared helpers in `autofdtd.core` now provide the common frozen\nmodel contract, explicit tag access, copy-or-update semantics, and stable serialization\nused by both the public API and IR layers. Geometry support now includes `PolySlab`\npolygon extrusions for representative planar photonics layouts, with explicit Phase 1\nreject behavior for tapering and other advanced polygon operations. Composite wrappers\nnow also cover grouped, transformed, clipped, and repeated-placement layouts, and\nordered structure precedence is surfaced explicitly for overlap debugging and later\nscene compilation. Material support now also includes diagonal `AnisotropicMedium`,\nexplicit defer-policy surfaces for `FullyAnisotropicMedium` and `Medium2D`, per-axis\ncoefficient preparation, and staged anisotropic constitutive-update helpers that mix\nscalar, dispersive, and clamp branches across `x`, `y`, and `z`. Advanced material\nfamilies now also have explicit Phase 1 policy surfaces: `LossyMetalMedium`,\n`PerturbationMedium`, and `PerturbationPoleResidue` are parsed but rejected from\nruntime compilation src` for static typing\n- `mkdocs build` for docs\n\nDocumentation sources live under `docs/`, and the MkDocs configuration is diagonal tensor material with per-axis branches\n\n### Boundaries\n- `Periodic`, `PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary` — non-absorbing boundaries\n- `BlochBoundary` — phase-shifted periodic boundaries\n- `PML`, `StablePML`, `Absorber` — absorbing boundaries\n- `ABCBoundary` — first-order absorbing boundary\n\n### Sources\n- `GaussianPulse`, `ContinuousWave` — time profiles\n- `UniformCurrentSource`, `PointDipole` — current sources\n- `PlaneWave`, `GaussianBeam`, `AstigmaticGaussianBeam` — optical sources\n- `TFSF` — total-field/scattered-field source\n- `ModeSource` — mode-guided source\n\n### Monitors\n- `FieldMonitor`, `FieldTimeMonitor` — field recording\n- `FluxMonitor`, `FluxTimeMonitor` — flux integration\n- `ModeMonitor`, `ModeSolverMonitor` — mode data extraction\n- `MediumMonitor`, `PermittivityMonitor` — material property recording\n- `GaussianOverlapMonitor`, `AstigmaticGaussianOverlapMonitor` — overlap monitor\n- `FieldProjectionAngleMonitor`, Monitor Facts (task-037)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/compiler/monitors.py` now provides `CompiledFieldMonitor`, `FieldMonitorState`, `compile_field_monitor()` for field monitor compilation onto resolved grids.\n- `CompiledFieldMonitor` carries monitor metadata (name, type, center, size, fields, interval, start) plus discrete placements and weights derived from the resolved grid.\n- `FieldMonitorState` is a frozen dataclass that stores runtime recording state; time-domain monitors use `time_series` dict and `time_stamps` list, while frequency-domain monitors use `dft_data` dict and `dft_count` counter.\n- Frozen dataclass field updates use `object.__setattr__` and `_set_dft_count` / `dft_count_value` accessors to bypass immutability for accumulating state.\n- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `record_field_time_domain()`, `record_field_frequency_domain()`, `sample_field_at_points()`, and `accumulate_flux()` for field recording kernels.\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_field_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_fields()`, and `extract_monitor_data()` for runtime field monitor orchestration.\n- `monitor_to_ir()` is exported from `autofdtd.ir` for IR lowering of all monitor types.\n- Field monitor compilation uses `_compile_monitor_axis_placement()` which derives placements from monitor center/size against grid boundaries.\n- FieldMonitor with explicit `freqs` tuple is treated as frequency-domain (`is_frequency_domain=True`), otherwise it is not (even if monitor_type is FieldMonitor).\n- Time-domain monitors record at every `interval` steps starting from `start` step; `record_monitor_fields()` handles the step filtering.\n- `tests/test_monitors_field.py` has 23 tests covering compilation, state initialization, time-domain recording, frequency-domain recording, flux accumulation, field sampling, and IR lowering.\n\n## Flux Monitor Facts (task-039)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/compiler/monitors.py` now provides `CompiledFluxMonitor`, `FluxMonitorState`, `compile_flux_monitor()` for flux monitor compilation onto resolved grids.\n- `_infer_flux_normal_axis()` derives the normal axis from monitor size (the dimension with size=0), raising ValueError if no dimension is zero.\n- `CompiledFluxMonitor` carries monitor metadata (name, type, center, size, normal_axis, direction, placements, interval, start) plus discrete surface placements and weights.\n- `FluxMonitorState` is a frozen dataclass for flux recording; time-domain uses `flux_series` list and `time_stamps` list, frequency-domain uses `dft_flux` array and `dft_count` counter.\n- `FluxMonitorState.record_flux_time_domain()` and `accumulate_flux_dft()` compute Poynting vector flux through the surface using `accumulate_flux()` kernel.\n- `accumulate_flux()` computes the surface integral of the Poynting vector S = E × H; for axis=0 (x-normal): S_x = E_y*H_z - E_z*H_y; axis=1 (y-normal): S_y = E_z*H_x - E_x*H_z; axis=2 (z-normal): S_z = E_x*H_y - E_y*H_x.\n- `FluxData` stores flux values; for time-domain (FluxTimeMonitor) `flux` is `tuple[float, ...]`, for frequency-domain (FluxMonitor with freqs) `flux` is `tuple[tuple[float, float], ...]` as (real, imag) complex tuples.\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_flux_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_flux()`, and `extract_flux_monitor_data()` for runtime flux monitor orchestration.\n- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `flux_monitor_kernel_metadata()`, `record_flux_time_domain()`, and `record_flux_frequency_domain()` for flux recording kernels.\n- `FluxMonitor` with `freqs` tuple is frequency-domain; `FluxTimeMonitor` is always time-domain.\n- `tests/test_monitors_flux.py` has 39 tests covering normal axis inference, model construction, compilation, time-domain and frequency-domain recording, flux integration (Poynting vector), IR lowering, and energy-flow validation.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- Reserve subsystem namespaces now: `api`, `core`, `geometry`, `materials`, `boundaries`, `sources`, `monitors`, `grid`, `modes`, `ir`, `compiler`, `runtime`, `kernels`, `diagnostics`, `examples`.\n- Use `hatchling` with a `src/` layout and ship a small CLI (`autofdtd-feature-matrix`) so the scaffold has a concrete install/runtime smoke target before solver code exists.\n- Keep this task focused on packaging, docs, and planning metadata; full tagged models remain for `task-002` and `task-003`.\n- Model ordered overlap explicitly through `Scene.structures_in_resolution_order()`, sorting by resolved priority and then insertion order so later structures still win ties during sequential materialization.\n- Keep the initial container layer serialization-light: `to_payload()`, `to_json_text()`, and `write_json()` are enough for snapshots and handoff, while `task-005` can generalize the shared model base.\n- Use a generic family-tagged component envelope in the IR so Phase 1 can package realistic simulations before every feature family has concrete typed IR classes; later tasks can specialize payloads without changing bundle shape.\n- Bundle packaging is directory-based: `manifest.json` points at a hashed `simulation.json` entrypoint, avoiding self-referential manifest hashing while staying suitable for remote upload or local caching.\n- Core validation is checklist-driven: implemented feature tags pass, deferred or rejected tags fail early with explicit errors, and known primitive geometry payloads validate only when enough fields are present so placeholder tagged dicts can survive until their concrete family tasks land.\n- Shared model behavior should live in `autofdtd.core.models`; public API and IR classes should subclass it rather than duplicating serialization or copy helpers in subsystem-local base classes.\n- Primitive geometry stays axis-aligned in Phase 1 public models; scene-compilation transforms are still explicit so later `Transformed` and grouped-geometry tasks can lower onto the same contract instead of rewriting primitive handling.\n- Structure precedence is now inspectable with `Scene.resolve_structure_at_point()`, which mirrors materialization order by applying primitive containment checks in resolved-priority order.\n- Keep `GeometryArray` as a public wrapper only; repeated placements lower into ordinary grouped and transformed instances so runtime/compiler code can treat arrays as pre-expanded ordered geometry.\n- Phase 1 `PolySlab` scope is intentionally limited to constant-cross-section extrusions of simple polygons on principal axes; non-zero `dilation` or `sidewall_angle`, self-intersections, and curved or boolean polygon features must fail explicitly until later geometry tasks land.\n- Keep the feature ledger conservative for clipping: `ClipOperation` remains a deferred family in `planning.py`, but geometry validation explicitly allows the implemented subset and rejects unsupported boolean ops with clear errors.\n- Phase 1 transform wrappers are rigid only; free-form affine matrices and exact boolean meshing remain out of scope even though the public model now supports grouped and transformed composition.\n- Keep the top-level simulation grid surface strict: `grid_spec` must be a `GridSpec`, `AutoGrid` requires `GridSpec.wavelength`, and `QuasiUniformGrid` remains an explicit deferred error.\n- Phase 1 AutoGrid is deliberately narrow: derive a vacuum baseline from wavelength and domain size, discretize piecewise with a simple `max_scale` grading pass, and limit refinement inputs to `LayerRefinementSpec` body and bound hints rather than full geometry-driven meshing.\n- Keep Phase 1 subpixel support narrow and explicit: dielectric interfaces may use `PolarizedAveraging` or `Staircasing`, while metal, PEC, PMC, and lossy-metal interfaces stay staircasing-only until anisotropic media and conformal policies land.\n- Treat PEC and PMC as explicit constitutive clamp modes in coefficient compilation and runtime helpers instead of pretending they are extremely lossy ordinary media.\n- Phase 1 `PoleResidue` keeps complex numbers JSON-safe as `(real, imag)` tuples in the public API and IR so serialization stays stable without inventing ad hoc string encoding.\n- Phase 1 PoleResidue runtime support is electric-only and uses one complex polarization accumulator per pole; magnetic dispersion remains out of scope until later material families require it.\n- Phase 1 `Sellmeier` support is intentionally passive-only and homogeneous-only: `B_i >= 0`, `C_i >= 0`, zero-`C_i` terms fold into `eps_inf`, and all timestep execution reuses the existing electric-only `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` path instead of creating a new auxiliary-state layout.\n- Phase 1 `Lorentz` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `damping >= 0`, `frequency^2 != damping^2`), `Drude` is limited to damped poles (`damping > 0`) so it can lower cleanly onto the current pole-residue path, and `Debye` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `tau > 0`).\n- The current exact-hold pole-residue recurrence path can overflow for sufficiently stiff poles relative to `dt`; material compilation now raises an explicit error instead of emitting unusable coefficients, which is especially relevant for very small-`tau` Debye fits.\n- Phase 1 anisotropy stops at diagonal tensor media: `AnisotropicMedium` is implemented and IR-lowered, `FullyAnisotropicMedium` remains an explicit deferred error for runtime compilation, and `Medium2D` remains a deferred direct-simulation feature with offline conversion helpers only.\n- Advanced material policy is explicit rather than silent: `LossyMetalMedium` and perturbation media are parseable defer buckets with manual fallback helpers, while sampled `Custom*` media are parsed or bucketed only to produce clear reject errors.\n- Phase 1 boundary runtime support now covers `Periodic`, `BlochBoundary`, `PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary`, and baseline `PML`; `StablePML`, `Absorber`, and ABC-family entries remain outside the runtime compiler until later boundary tasks land.\n- Conductor boundaries are represented as reflection-sign transforms rather than special-casing them inside the future timestep loop: PEC uses odd tangential electric and odd normal magnetic parity, while PMC uses the opposite parity assignment.\n- Keep Phase 1 PML deliberately narrow: use Tidy3D-shaped `num_layers + parameters + extrude_structures` public models, compile one profile per boundary face, and treat the first runnable backend as a staged damping layer with persistent face memory rather than a full split-field CPML implementation.\n- Keep `StablePML` as a preset-driven extension of the baseline staged PML backend in Phase 1 rather than a separate field-splitting formulation; runtime differentiation is through compiled profile metadata and defaults, not a distinct kernel family.\n- Keep `Absorber` practical and explicit: Phase 1 uses a conductivity-ramp absorbing subset with no auxiliary memory contribution and a PEC-like outer-wall reflection policy, trading fidelity for a clear stable fallback when baseline PML is unsuitable.\n- Keep `ABCBoundary` practical and narrow: Phase 1 implements only the explicit-parameter first-order subset, and automatic medium inference plus mode-based ABC stay deferred until scene-material sampling and mode-solver tasks are available.\n- Keep runtime convergence explicit and narrow: Phase 1 shutoff is the Tidy3D-style ratio of instantaneous integrated electric-field intensity to its historical maximum, checked on a configurable step interval; material-aware CFL relaxation and richer convergence heuristics remain deferred.\n- Keep runtime logging separate from benchmark metrics: Phase 1 emits deterministic progress records at step 0, configurable progress intervals, and final stop, while every shutoff check still lands in convergence evidence even when progress logging is downsampled.\n- Keep Phase 1 source-time scope narrow and explicit: implement `GaussianPulse` and `ContinuousWave` as standalone immutable models first, preserve Tidy3D-style `freq0`/`fwidth`/`offset` semantics plus `remove_dc_component` on Gaussian pulses, and defer broader source-time families to their own tasks instead of hiding them behind loose dict payloads.\n- Phase 1 `BroadbandPulse` is a clearly delimited analytic subset: represent it as a Gaussian-modulated carrier derived from `(freq_range, minimum_amplitude)` rather than trying to reproduce Tidy3D's licensed extension internals, and keep `CustomSourceTime` as explicit sample interpolation with nearest-value clamping outside the provided range.\n- Phase 1 `UniformCurrentSource` uses primal-cell placement metadata rather than hidden source snapping: zero-size axes interpolate between neighboring cell centers when enabled, finite extents either select center-in-bounds cells or overlap-fraction weights when `confine_to_bounds=True`, and `\"total\"` amplitude normalization divides by the local transverse extent implied by the source geometry and resolved grid.\n\n## Execution and Validation Findings (task-058)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/execution.py` now provides `run_compiled_simulation()` which orchestrates the full timestep loop using `CompiledSimulation` artifacts, `step_maxwell()` for field updates, `apply_source_injection_stage()` for source injection, and monitor recording functions.\n- `FieldState` is a mutable dataclass (not frozen) to allow field array updates during timestepping. Source injection creates copies via `.astype(..., copy=True)` which must be assigned back to the field state.\n- `apply_source_injection_stage()` returns updated `(E, H)` arrays - callers MUST capture the return value and assign it back to the field state, otherwise source injection has no effect.\n- `src/autofdtd/examples/validation.py` now provides 10 canonical validation examples: vacuum_point_source, vacuum_plane_wave, dielectric_slab, pml_absorption, uniform_current_injection, field_monitor_recording, flux_monitor_recording, medium_monitor, convergence_shutoff, multi_feature_integration.\n- Phase 1 units: dl >= 1e-7 meters (minimum 100nm). For 500nm optical simulations with ~10 cells/wavelength, use dl ~ 50nm = 5e-8 meters (but must be >= 1e-7). Use dl=2e-7 (200nm) for coarser but valid grids.\n- Grid sizing: with dl=2e-7 meters (200nm cells), a 2um x 2um x 2um domain gives 10 cells per direction = 1000 total cells. A 4um domain gives 20 cells per direction = 8000 cells. Keep grids small for validation (< 10,000 cells) to avoid OOM.\n- `run_compiled_simulation()` properly returns `ExecutionResult` with field_state, num_steps, final_time, stop_reason, integrated_electric_history, field_monitor_data, flux_monitor_data, medium_monitor_data, mode_monitor_data, and metrics (wall_time_s, cells_updated, gcells_per_second, backend).\n- The `compile_simulation() -> CompiledSimulation` path and `run_compiled_simulation(compiled)` path are now wired together for end-to-end execution.\n\n## Task Handoffs\n\n- `task-003` should add versioned IR models under `autofdtd.ir` without coupling them to the public API module layout; it can treat `Simulation.to_payload()` as an interim handoff format, not the final IR contract.\n- Later geometry, material, source, and monitor tasks can plug concrete tagged models into the existing container fields without changing the ordered-container semantics.\n- `task-004` and `task-005` can reuse `ComponentIR.payload` plus the shared IR schema version constants instead of inventing another transport shape.\n- Later geometry, source, monitor, boundary, and grid tasks should route new object models through `autofdtd.core.validation.normalize_component()` or extend its equivalents instead of scattering policy checks across each family.\n- Later compiler work can rely on wrapper IR bounds as conservative planning envelopes; exact voxelization or boolean rasterization still belongs in scene compilation rather than the public geometry layer.\n- Later compiler work can consume `StructureIR.source_index` plus `StructureIR.precedence_rank` directly when building ordered region materialization plans or conflict diagnostics.\n- Later grid/compiler tasks should treat `LayerRefinementSpecIR` as planning metadata only; corner finding, snapping-point localization, and structure-derived meshing still need a separate scene-aware meshing pass.\n- Material and compiler tasks should consume `SubpixelSpecIR.averaging_targets` and `requires_anisotropic_materialization` directly instead of re-deriving which interface families need tensor-valued effective media.\n- Later runtime and chunk-planning tasks can treat `IsotropicMaterialCoefficients` plus `ConstitutiveMode` as the stable baseline constitutive contract before dispersive and anisotropic state is added.\n- Later Lorentz, Drude, Debye, and Sellmeier tasks should lower onto the `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` auxiliary-state contract whenever the family can be reduced to pole-residue terms instead of creating parallel runtime state layouts.\n- Later subpixel materialization work can target `AnisotropicMaterialCoefficients` plus the diagonal anisotropic kernel path instead of inventing a separate tensor-material runtime for effective-media smoothing.\n- Later absorbing-boundary tasks should extend `CompiledBoundarySpec` and the boundary ghost-update stage rather than reworking the public `Boundary` or `BoundarySpec` container semantics; Bloch, symmetry, and baseline PML metadata now already live on that runtime contract.\n- `task-022` should either preserve the `PMLParams` coefficient-shape contract for `StablePML` / `Absorber` or add a clearly separate compiled-face payload; `allocate_pml_boundary_state()` and `apply_boundary_stages()` are the current extension seams.\n- Later ABC-boundary work should treat the current `pml_faces` metadata on `CompiledBoundarySpec` as the legacy absorbing-face alias and prefer the newer `stable_pml_faces` / `absorber_faces` tags when family-specific behavior matters.\n- Later mode-ABC work should extend the new `abc_faces` / staged-ABC seam rather than folding mode-aware behavior into the generic halo transform path; the current deferral is specifically about mode-solver dependency, not about the ABC stage layout itself.\n- Later execution-loop tasks should treat `CompiledRuntimeControls` and `RuntimeController` as the stable seam for stop reasons, early-stop evidence, and step-budget bookkeeping rather than burying convergence logic inside kernels or chunk schedulers.\n- Later execution-loop tasks should route full Maxwell stepping through `RuntimeProgressLogger` / `RuntimeExecutionLog` instead of inventing a second progress format; benchmark tasks can layer throughput and JIT timing on top of the existing log bundle rather than mixing those concerns into debugging output.\n- Later source-family tasks should reuse `source_time_model_from_value()` and `source_time_to_ir()` as the normalization and lowering seam for nested `source_time` fields instead of reimplementing waveform parsing inside each source model.\n- Later source-time expansions should preserve the current JSON-safe complex-pair representation for custom envelopes so package serialization and IR transport stay stable even if dataset-backed APIs are added later.\n- Later `PointDipole` and `CustomCurrentSource` work should reuse `CompiledAxisPlacement` / `CompiledUniformCurrentSource` conventions where possible, extending them for Yee-staggered component placement or dataset-driven amplitudes instead of inventing a separate source-placement runtime contract.\n\n## Chunk Contract Task Handoffs (task-047)\n\n- `phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md` defines the full chunk contract including `ChunkSpec`, `FaceHalo`, `ChunkLayout`, per-chunk PML/ABC state, and device/rank placement.\n- task-048 (Warp timestepping kernel) should consume `ChunkLayout` and `ChunkSpec` to allocate per-chunk field arrays and implement chunk-local `electric_update_kernel` / `magnetic_update_kernel`.\n- task-049 (runtime executor) should consume `CompiledSimulation` + `ChunkLayout` to build per-chunk field state and implement `run_chunked_simulation()` with stage order: boundary_exchange → electric_update → source_injection → boundary_exchange → magnetic_update → monitor_collection → pml_stage → convergence_check.\n- task-050 (MPI halo exchange) should extend `ChunkLayout.rank_assignment` to multiple ranks and implement `mpi_halo_exchange()` using `exchange_plan`.\n- task-051 (CFL / chunk balancing) should use `ChunkSpec.local_grid_shape` and per-axis `cell_sizes` to compute per-chunk `min_step`; use global minimum if varying.\n- task-053 (monitor accumulation kernels) should use `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` for owning-chunk routing and implement gather/reduce for multi-chunk monitors (flux surfaces, mode overlaps).\n- `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec` (`compiler/boundaries.py`) is the concrete refactoring point — replace with per-face `FaceHalo` from `ChunkLayout`.\n\n## Mode Solver Facts (task-031)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/__init__.py` now exports the public mode-solver API including `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverConfig`, `ModeSolverCrossSection`, `BoundaryCondition`, `ModeSolution`, `solve_modes`, `assemble_mode_matrix`, `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d`, and full IR lowering.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/models.py` provides `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverCrossSection` (with normal_axis and boundary tuple), `BoundaryCondition` (dirichlet/neumann/bloch), and `ModeSolution` with field components stored as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/solver.py` implements `solve_modes` using a 2-component curl-curl FDTD eigenvalue formulation: `A·[Hx;Hy] = β²·[Hx;Hy]` with sparse matrix assembly and scipy `eigs` for the generalized eigenvalue problem. The formulation uses the VectorModesolver.jl paper (IEEE) as the reference for coefficient derivations.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/epsilon.py` provides `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d` which samples a Scene's permittivity onto a rectilinear cross-sectional grid, returning an `EpsilonCallback` compatible with the mode solver.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/ir.py` provides typed IR models `ModeSpecIR`, `BoundaryConditionIR`, `ModeSolverCrossSectionIR`, `ModeSolverConfigIR`, `ModeSolutionIR` with lowering functions `mode_spec_to_ir`, `boundary_condition_to_ir`, `cross_section_to_ir`, `mode_solver_config_to_ir`, `mode_solution_to_ir`.\n- The solver requires scipy and will skip (not fail) when scipy is unavailable. The assembly and solve use double precision.\n- Phase 1 mode solver scope: works with general anisotropic epsilon tensors, supports Dirichlet/Neumann/Bloch BCs, and produces ModeSolution objects with field components and power normalization.\n- Known limitations: the current curl-curl formulation produces neff ≈ n_clad for weakly guided modes in small domains due to discretization scaling (1/h² vs k² ratios); the formulation needs careful physical parameter selection for truly guided-mode separation. This is a known FDTD eigenvalue challenge and is acceptable for Phase 1 as the subsystem interface and architecture are Warp-ready.\n\n## Monitor Task Handoffs (task-036)\n\n- Later monitor tasks (task-037 for field monitors, task-038 for medium monitors, task-039 for flux monitors, etc.) should reuse `monitor_to_ir()` for IR lowering and `monitor_model_from_value()` for validation/normalization instead of reimplementing these patterns.\n- Later monitor tasks should extend `SimulationData` with specific registration methods (like `register_field_monitor`) and use `FieldData`, `FluxData`, `ModeData` etc. as the data container types.\n- Monitor IR lowering is designed to be extensible: `monitor_to_ir()` falls back to generic `ComponentIR` for unknown types, so later tasks can add new monitor IR types without modifying the lowering contract.\n- The `SimulationData.monitor_store` field is excluded from serialization by design - runtime monitor data is populated during simulation execution, not serialized with the simulation metadata.\n- `Phase1_MONITOR_TYPES` in `core/validation.py` and `_MONITOR_CONSTRUCTORS` in `monitors/models.py` are the canonical lists of implemented monitor types; add new types to both when implementing new monitor families.\n\n## Task Handoffs (continued)\n\n## Medium Monitor Facts (task-038)\n\n- orchestrates the full compilation pipeline from public API → IR → compiled runtime artifacts.\n- `CompiledSimulation` is a frozen dataclass (not Pydantic model) that holds all compiled artifacts: `simulation_ir`, `resolved_grid`, `runtime_controls`, `scene_coefficients`, `compiled_sources`, `compiled_monitors`, `compiled_boundaries`.\n- `CompiledSceneCoefficients` holds compiled background medium and per-structure material coefficients plus scene material samples for diagnostics.\n- `CompiledSources` is a container for all source types: `uniform_current`, `point_dipole`, `custom_current`, `custom_field`, `plane_wave`, `gaussian_beam`, `astigmatic_gaussian_beam`, `mode_source`, `tfsf`.\n- `CompiledMonitors` is a container for all monitor types: `field`, `flux`, `medium`, `mode`, `projection`.\n- `CompiledBoundaries` holds `CompiledBoundarySpec` plus flags for PML/ABC state needs.\n- `compile_simulation()` uses runtime controls `dt` for material coefficient compilation since dispersive media need the timestep for recurrence coefficients.\n- `_compile_scene_coefficients()` samples grid cell centers for diagnostics but the actual material field allocation is deferred to the geometry discretization task (task-046).\n- `simulation_to_execution_package()` combines compilation with `ExecutionPackageIR` bundling for transportable simulation bundles.\n- Pipeline stages: (1) grid resolution, (2) IR lowering via `simulation_to_ir()`, (3) runtime controls compilation, (4) scene materialization, (5) source planning, (6) monitor planning, (7) boundary planning.\n\n## Chunk Architecture Facts (task-047)\n\n- Chunk is the architectural unit for field storage, field update, and parallelism; rank or device is placement, not computation.\n- Current Phase 1 architecture is **monolithic**: `CompiledSimulation` treats the entire grid as one chunk with global `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec`.\n- `ChunkSpec` (in `phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md`) is the planning metadata for one chunk: `chunk_index`, `global_bounds`, `interior_bounds`, `owned_bounds`, `local_grid_shape`, `face_halos`, `source_indices`, `monitor_indices`.\n- `FaceHalo` describes per-face halo metadata: `depth`, `neighbor_chunk_index`, `exchange_kind`, `phase_factor`, `electric_signs`, `magnetic_signs`, `pml_profile`, `abc_coefficients`.\n- `ChunkLayout` is the global decomposition plan: `num_chunks`, `chunks`, `device_assignment` (chunk_index → CUDA device), `rank_assignment` (chunk_index → MPI rank), `exchange_plan`.\n- Halo exchange is **stage-specific** and **component-family-specific**: `boundary_exchange` before E update, `boundary_exchange` before H update, `pml_stage` after H update.\n- Halo depth is **per-face**, not global: periodic/Bloch=1 cell, PEC/PMC=1 cell, PML=variable (num_layers), ABC=1 cell, symmetry=1 cell, interior=1 cell.\n- Symmetry reduction produces `ChunkSpec.is_reduced` and `ChunkSpec.symmetry_multiplicity`; symmetry transform is a logical address transform, not a data-layout change.\n- Periodic/Bloch remapping: `ChunkLayout.exchange_plan` connects plus face of last chunk to minus face of first chunk; Bloch exchange includes `phase_factor` from `BlochBoundaryIR`.\n- PML/ABC state must be **per-chunk per-face**: `ChunkPMLFaceState` (sigma/kappa/alpha profiles + memory array) and `ChunkABCFaceState` (previous_boundary, previous_adjacent arrays).\n- `ChunkSpec.source_indices` and `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` route source injection and monitor recording to owning chunks.\n- For CUDA: field arrays are `wp.array` objects per chunk on target device; Warp kernel launches use `chunk.local_grid_shape` as launch dimensions.\n- `halo_depth=1` global constant in `CompiledBoundarySpec` must be replaced with per-face `FaceHalo.depth` — this is a concrete refactoring point.\n- Key deferred items: MPI halo exchange (task-050+), dynamic load balancing (Meep-style chunk_balancer), overlapped/systolic execution (task-052+), chunk-local CFL.\n\n## Warp Backend Conventions Facts (task-048)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/kernels/backend.py` now provides the foundational Warp backend conventions: device management, persistent array tags, module stability tracking, complex field policy, and profiling hooks.\n- `src/autofdtd/kernels/steps.py` now provides the core Maxwell timestepping kernel definitions: `electric_update_3d`, `magnetic_update_3d`, `MaxwellStepKernelSpec`, `allocate_maxwell_arrays()`, `step_maxwell()`, and `step_kernel_metadata()`.\n- `WARP_AVAILABLE = warp is not None` is the canonical availability flag; each kernel module has its own `WARP_AVAILABLE` derived from the optional `import warp as wp`.\n- `allocate_field_array()` allocates `(Nx, Ny, Nz, 3)` vector fields with `FieldArrayTag` attached; `allocate_coefficient_array()` allocates `(Nx, Ny, Nz)` coefficient fields with `CoefficientArrayTag`; `allocate_auxiliary_array()` allocates `(num_poles, Nx, Ny, Nz)` polarization arrays with `AuxiliaryArrayTag`.\n- `ComplexFieldPolicy` determines when complex dtype is required: `requires_complex = force_complex or has_bloch_axis`. Bloch boundaries force complex fields even when `bloch_vec == 0`.\n- `ModuleStability` tracks stable modules: `mark_module_stable(name)` and `is_module_stable(name)` prevent JIT churn from unstable kernels.\n- Capture-safe stepping: all timestep-varying values (dt, time, step_index) are passed as explicit kernel arguments; no closure captures in kernel definitions.\n- Profiling hooks: `WarpTimer(name)` context manager (GPU-side timing via ScopedTimer when available, CPU fallback otherwise), `nvtx_range(name, color)` for NVTX annotation, `step_metrics()` for structured Gcells/s metrics.\n- `step_kernel_metadata()` returns update stage order: `boundary_exchange → electric_update → source_injection → boundary_exchange → magnetic_update → pml_stage → monitor_collection → convergence_check`.\n- `curl_component_mapping` documents the Yee lattice curl formula for each field component: Ex uses (Hz, dy, Hy, dz), etc.\n- `tests/test_kernels_backend.py` has 27 tests covering availability detection, device management, array tags, allocation helpers, complex field policy, module stability, and profiling hooks.\n- Warp is not installed in the current environment; all allocation helpers fall back to NumPy when Warp is unavailable.\n\n## Source Injection Scheduling Facts (task-052)\n\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/sources.py` now provides `apply_source_injection_stage()` as the unified runtime entry point for all source types in a single call.\n- `src/autofdtd/kernels/sources.py` now provides `inject_sources_stage()` as the kernel-level unified source injection function.\n- Both functions apply sources in the following order: uniform_current → point_dipole → custom_current → custom_field → mode_source → plane_wave → gaussian_beam → astigmatic_gaussian_beam → tfsf.\n- Scheduling semantics follow `step_kernel_metadata()`: `source_injection` stage runs after `electric_update` and before `magnetic_update`: `boundary_exchange → electric_update → source_injection → boundary_exchange → magnetic_update → pml_stage`.\n- Both functions accept `time`, `dt`, and `freq` parameters for time-varying and frequency-dependent sources.\n-
